## Current roadmap generation
- `v2` is now the active roadmap generation.
- `R0` through `R7` remain the closed foundation workstreams from the completed first roadmap.
- `R8` through `R14` are the active/planned workstreams for the post-modernization roadmap.
- [ROADMAP_V2.md](ROADMAP_V2.md) is the detailed companion roadmap; this file remains the canonical live status board.
- Long-term horizon goals beyond the active `R8`..`R14` lanes are also tracked in [ROADMAP_V2.md](ROADMAP_V2.md), but they are intentionally gated behind the “make the tool state-of-the-art and very stable first” rule.
- The current saved `H1` guidance is that a future Rust implementation should likely start in this same repository beside the Perl reference implementation, not in a separate repository plus submodule arrangement, unless release cadence or ownership later diverge enough to justify a split.
- The current saved `H3` guidance is that any future HDL-to-`.fsm` direction should be framed as bounded synthesizable-RTL import / intent recovery, not as a promise of exact reverse compilation, with `fsmgen`-generated `SystemVerilog` as the most honest first round-trip target and richer handwritten hierarchy/generate/macro recovery treated as deliberate later widening after real frontend semantic compilation plus elaboration, even when no full backend compile/synthesis flow is involved; design/probe work can begin before that becomes the main implementation lane, but serious implementation should stay behind the current forward/backend cleanup and language-contract stabilization so the reverse lane targets a steadier semantic surface; the saved architecture direction is also to keep separate syntax/front-end trees but converge both directions onto a shared semantic middle centered on `Intent HIR` and `Lowered RTL IR` rather than two unrelated semantic stacks.
- The current saved `H4` guidance is that any future TRM / protocol-spec / `Markdown` to `.fsm` direction should be treated as a distinct intent-capture lane rather than being collapsed into HDL import, with a likely `PDF -> .md -> normalized spec IR -> recovered protocol roles/transactions/timing rules/invariants -> .fsm + capture report` shape, explicit confidence/heuristic/ambiguity residue reporting, and early bounded outputs such as requester/completer roles, protocol checkers/monitors, reusable assertions, and optional `.fsm` composition/testbench harnesses for protocols like `APB`, `AMBA`, `AXI`, `I2C`, and `I2S`; the current saved method guidance is to start from normalized Markdown, work actor-first, build dossier/catalog/sheet/assertion/abstraction/validation artifacts before emission, keep source facts, derived machine rules, local design decisions, and explicit abstractions separate, and preserve explicit stage gates around interfaces, phases, invariants/contracts/gates/assertions, abstractions, decomposition, emission, and verification assets; the stronger long-term target is an almost-fully-staged automated “executable PDF” flow that can eventually emit `.fsm` sets plus harness/testbench surfaces, scenario/test intent, verification plans, functional-coverage plans, and an honesty-preserving capture report; [docs/INTENT_CAPTURE_AXI_CASE_STUDY.md](docs/INTENT_CAPTURE_AXI_CASE_STUDY.md) is now the detailed saved case-study reference for that lane; this is worth design/probe work and future library seeding, but it should not interrupt the active forward/backend cleanup lane.
- The current saved hierarchy-elaboration guidance is that future `.fsm` design hierarchies should be authored bottom-up but compiled from the top root, with `fsmgen top.fsm` recursively realizing non-leaf composition nodes and leaf implementation nodes until the full design is emitted; source reuse may make the authored graph a DAG, but elaboration should still produce an instance tree, and internal non-leaf reusable composition modules should eventually become first-class authored artifacts under the broader `R11` reusable/composition lane.
- The current saved reusable-library guidance is that any future standard-library lane should start as ordinary reusable `.fsm` assets flowing through the normal parser/IR/emitter path, with a small curated gold set before any broader primitive zoo or privileged builtins.
- The current saved semantic-parameter guidance is that any future parameterization lane should use explicit semantic parameters plus explicit override binding that survives into IR, not a text/template preprocessor hidden behind clever syntax.
- The current saved execution-cadence guidance is that implementation should alternate more deliberately between consolidation/debt-reduction slices and visibly user-facing capability slices; long uninterrupted cleanup streaks should not be the default, and after a cleanup slice the next honest move should usually be a feature/contract/diagnostic slice unless more cleanup is still the clear blocker.
- The current saved SPECFORGE alignment guidance is tracked in [docs/SPECFORGE_FEEDBACK_RESPONSE.md](docs/SPECFORGE_FEEDBACK_RESPONSE.md): FSMGen accepts the direction of becoming a precise `.fsm` contract authority for downstream tools, with strict mode as the generated-`.fsm` target, the first bounded `--capability-manifest` now shipped from support-accounting truth, stable diagnostic-code ownership and bounded check-only JSON diagnostics now shipped, the first bounded normalized semantic JSON export now shipped, stronger clock/reset metadata, and later checked actor/channel/role/temporal/provenance metadata staged behind support accounting and `R13`.

## Update rule
- Update this file before every commit if the completed task changes:
  - any workstream status,
  - any workstream deliverables,
  - the `Done` summary,
  - the `Left` summary,
  - or the current active lane.
- Whenever any workstream status or the current active lane changes:
  - refresh this board first,
  - log the change in `CHANGES.md`,
  - and display the current live status snapshot in the user-facing close-out for that task.
- Whenever the commit workflow runs, display the current live status snapshot from this board in the user-facing close-out, even if no status changed.
- Allowed status values are exactly:
  - `done`
  - `mostly done`
  - `in progress`
  - `not started`
- Every workstream must state:
  - `Description`: a brief statement of what the phase does and why it exists,
  - `Deliverables`: the concrete outputs required for the phase to count as complete,
  - `Status`: current achievement level against those deliverables,
  - `Done`: what is already landed,
  - `Left`: what still remains,
  - `Exit criteria`: the completion boundary.
- Every user-facing live-status snapshot derived from this board must show, for each `Rj`:
  - `Status`
  - `Description`
- When a phase has meaningful open sub-steps, the snapshot should also show brief sub-bullets for:
  - the current active lane,
  - any phase whose status changed in the completed task,
  - and any other phase where the next step is important to understand current progress.

## Status scale
- `done`
  - All listed `Deliverables` are complete.
  - `Exit criteria` are met.
  - Only incidental bugfixes or unrelated future reuse may remain.
- `mostly done`
  - Most listed `Deliverables` are complete.
  - Only a bounded finish-up lane remains.
- `in progress`
  - Some listed `Deliverables` are complete.
  - Multiple meaningful slices still remain.
- `not started`
  - The listed `Deliverables` are not yet implemented.
  - Notes or terminology may exist, but they do not count as implementation progress.

Failed diagnostics include a nested `support_accounting` object, the classifier prefers the most specific matching expected-error pattern, [t/300-check-json-regression-corpus.t](t/300-check-json-regression-corpus.t) proves every current `expected_failure` entry emits its exact stable code and matched corpus contract through `--check-json`, [t/301-check-json-supported-corpus.t](t/301-check-json-supported-corpus.t) proves every current `supported_smoke` entry succeeds through `--check-json` while every current `strict_supported` entry succeeds through `--strict --check-json` without HDL emission, and [t/312-check-diagnostics-contract.t](t/312-check-diagnostics-contract.t) now locks the bounded top-level, producer-object, source-object, success-support-accounting, success-result, and failure-diagnostic promises directly. Future check-JSON schema stabilization should continue widening only where fields are backed by support-accounting truth.

The backend now explicitly declares generated enable wires, sizes common multi-bit reset literals, infers direct widths from static slices/selectors/guards, propagates later selector evidence back through simple whole-signal assignment edges before interface generation, emits width-safe reduction truthiness in flattened one-bit enable predicates, preserves CoreAST signal slices through enable-graph rendering, and preserves right-nested same-precedence arithmetic grouping before final emission. This lane treats Verilator as the generated-SV validity gate and Yosys as a structural-netlist sanity gate; ABC-specific timeout/mapping behavior is deliberately deferred to a later dedicated lane.

  - SPECFORGE feedback has now been accepted as a tracked FSMGen-side integration alignment record in [docs/SPECFORGE_FEEDBACK_RESPONSE.md](docs/SPECFORGE_FEEDBACK_RESPONSE.md); it does not make FSMGen responsible for SPECFORGE's PDF/IntentIR recovery, but it does steer `R12`/`R10`/`R13` toward capability-manifest widening, stable diagnostic codes, check-only JSON diagnostics, normalized semantic JSON export, stronger reset/clock metadata, and later checked actor/channel/temporal/provenance metadata.
  - The recent `R11` alias slice shipped explicit external-RTL instance aliases: `(?rtl:module_name)` remains the shorthand where the instance and module/interface contract share one name, while `(?rtl:instance_name module_name)` realizes a distinct instance from the shared `(?rtlif:module_name ...)` contract and lets one `.rtlif` root back several instances.
  - The external-RTL parameter/generic override contract is now shipped: `.rtlif` may declare scalar or bounded aggregate `(params (NAME default_value) ...)` entries, `?rtl` instances may override those declared names through semantic `(params (NAME value) ...)` blocks, aggregate overrides are checked against the `.rtlif` default's inferred aggregate shape, and validated values now flow through `Instance`, `RealizedInstance`, structural RTL IR, and SystemVerilog `#(...)` instance-parameter emission. VHDL generic-map lowering remains follow-up work.
  - External-RTL parameter override values now also reuse composition-top and imported-package semantic symbols before planning: local constants, enum members, whole aggregate roots, and package-qualified roots such as `param_pkg.RESET_A5` are resolved after package imports populate `TopSymbols`, while unresolved override value symbols still abort before realization or HDL emission.
  - Package-backed `.rtlif` declaration defaults are now shipped for the bounded external-RTL metadata lane too: declaration defaults may use package-qualified symbols from the consuming composition source's imported packages, preserving scalar/list/record shape for override validation, while unresolved default symbols abort before realization or HDL emission and unqualified top-local names deliberately remain out of the reusable metadata default scope.
  - The matching direct-root parameter cleanup is now shipped too: `(+params ...)` uses the neutral `FSM::ParameterValueSupport` owner, accepts the same bounded scalar integer formats and packed aggregate payloads as external RTL metadata, can now reuse resolved direct-root constants, enum members, aggregate constant roots, direct `+define` literals, and same-root direct `+params` as parameter values, resolves parameter-to-parameter references as an acyclic declaration-order-independent graph, records direct parameter metadata on the semantic module so Intent HIR exposes direct-root `parameter_names`, preserves direct parameter uses as `ParameterRef` AST leaves instead of literal-substituting their defaults, and emits direct SystemVerilog module `#(...)` parameter declarations.
  - Generated-child parameterization is now shipped for the bounded `?fsmc` / `?dtc` lane: a generated-child instance may use one semantic `(params (NAME value) ...)` block, the resolver handles deferred top/package symbols for all child kinds, `GeneratedChildRealizer` validates override names against the realized child's direct `+params` declarations and checks aggregate override shape against the child default, and valid values preserve through the composition plan, Intent HIR, structural RTL IR, and current SystemVerilog `#(...)` generated-child instance emission. The first bounded expression-valued scalar parameter/generic slice is now shipped too for direct `+params`, `.rtlif` defaults, external `?rtl` overrides, and generated-child overrides. Parameters/generics remain scalar-or-aggregate semantic values, and the first bounded aggregate operator slice is now shipped for leafwise `+` / `-` / `*` / `/` / `%` / `&` / `|` / `^` plus `add` / `sub` / `mul` / `div` / `mod` / `and` / `or` / `xor` across matching list/record aggregate shapes, folded before HDL lowering with fixed-width unsigned leaf overflow, underflow, and divide/modulo-by-zero rejection. VHDL generic-map lowering and richer expression domains remain follow-up work.
  - The direct-root language surface now treats canonical init/default metadata and width declarations as expression-bearing intent slots: `(:= (signal value))` is the strict-safe canonical init/reset form, its `value` may be a nested Lisp-ish expression over constants/enums/params/aggregate scalar leaves, legacy compact `(:= signal=value)` remains default-mode compatibility residue, and `+size` width entries may use positive integer constant expressions over the same semantic leaves before generation. The shared scalar literal path now also accepts common decimal, `0d`, based SystemVerilog, `0x`, `0b`, `0o`, and intent-level sized spellings such as `5'23`, `8'-10`, `8'-0xA`, `8'-0b1010`, and `20'x1` consistently across direct/composition/package constants, positive integer scalar width symbols, and direct `+size` constant-expression terms through the shared `IntegerLiteralSupport` owner.
  - The direct SystemVerilog post-flattening assembly owner now makes the stage-before-declaration ordering explicit: [perl/FSM/HDL/FlattenedDT/Backend/SystemVerilog/PostFlatteningAssemblySupport.pm](perl/FSM/HDL/FlattenedDT/Backend/SystemVerilog/PostFlatteningAssemblySupport.pm) prepares the consolidated intermediate stage before internal declarations so prescan/factorization-discovered helpers are declared, while still emitting the rendered stage text after enable conditions in the final HDL.
  - The README/session-bootstrap import-tree sanity pass was rerun after the latest support-accounting and manifest-contract slices, including the new bounded shared `SupportAccountingMatchContract`: [docs/BIN_FSMGEN_IMPORT_TREE.md](docs/BIN_FSMGEN_IMPORT_TREE.md) now measures `145` reachable project files and `144` reachable `.pm` packages, updates the reachable `Support` family to `23`, records [perl/FSM/Support/SupportAccountingMatchContract.pm](perl/FSM/Support/SupportAccountingMatchContract.pm) alongside the other manifest-facing/report-facing contract owners, and keeps [perl/FSM/Package/IntegerLiteralSupport.pm](perl/FSM/Package/IntegerLiteralSupport.pm), [perl/FSM/ParameterValueSupport.pm](perl/FSM/ParameterValueSupport.pm), [perl/FSM/HDL/FlattenedDT/Backend/SystemVerilog/ConsolidatedIntermediateStageSupport.pm](perl/FSM/HDL/FlattenedDT/Backend/SystemVerilog/ConsolidatedIntermediateStageSupport.pm), and [perl/FSM/HDL/FlattenedDT/Backend/SystemVerilog/PostFlatteningAssemblySupport.pm](perl/FSM/HDL/FlattenedDT/Backend/SystemVerilog/PostFlatteningAssemblySupport.pm) called out as the live shared-owner checkpoints.
  - The recent `R12` slices promoted the hardened `.rtlif` output-direction system-role rejection, duplicate-port declaration rejection, unsupported port type rejection, invalid token-shape rejection, non-positive width rejection, missing-root rejection, empty-root rejection, nested-structure rejection, and duplicate embedded-root rejection into the regression corpus, each using isolated sidecar module names where sidecars are involved so the existing `uart_tx` missing-sidecar and focused diagnostic fixtures keep their exact meaning.
  - Documentation strategy note: the guide is now considered large enough that the split should be planned concretely, and that plan now lives in [docs/BOOK_PLAN.md](docs/BOOK_PLAN.md) with `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` as the intended landing page / table of contents for a future chaptered `docs/book/` set.
  - Documentation strategy note: when `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` becomes too large, split it into a book-like docs set with one Markdown file per major topic, keep `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` as the landing page / table of contents, and lean on realistic examples throughout so users can master FSMGen incrementally.
  - The next typed structural-handoff slice is now also shipped: inferred composition carrier nets now preserve `declared_type_name` / canonical `declared_type_spec` when they are driven by one typed child-output family, so internal structural connectivity no longer drops named aggregate/scalar type identity at the net boundary.
  - The next typed explicit-actual slice is now also shipped: whole named aggregate actual roots such as `=FRAME` now preserve enough inferred list/record shape to block width-equal but aggregate-shape-incompatible typed direct targets on the direct explicit-actual path, instead of validating only by packed width.
  - The matching typed direct-root slice is now also shipped: whole aggregate RHS assignments such as `(OUT = FRAME)` now preserve enough aggregate provenance to block width-equal but aggregate-shape-incompatible typed `+size` targets before SystemVerilog emission, instead of relying on packed width alone on the direct assignment path.
  - The next typed source-expression slice is now also shipped: source-side top expressions and child expressions now preserve enough inferred aggregate/list shape to block width-equal but aggregate-shape-incompatible typed direct targets on the explicit-link path, instead of validating concat/repeat/bit-slice/whole-signal expressions only by packed width.
  - The next typed structural-binding slice is now also shipped: realized composition-plan instance bindings and exported `structural_rtl_ir` instance bindings now preserve `connection_type_name` plus canonical `connection_type_spec` whenever one typed source contract is already known, so typed signal bindings, source expressions, and whole aggregate actual roots no longer drop back to width-only metadata at the child-binding handoff.
  - The next emitted-aggregate-lowering slices are now shipped on both live Verilog-family generated surfaces: structural SystemVerilog emission and the direct generated-module SystemVerilog backend now synthesize backend-owned local packed typedefs for declared aggregate aliases, so typed top ports, typed structural nets, direct module ports, and direct internal/helper declarations no longer flatten all the way back to raw vector declarations at the final emitted HDL boundary.
  - The next composition aggregate-expression slice is now shipped: explicit `?toplink` source expressions can read declared aggregate top-port members/items such as `in_frame.tag` / `in_frame.payload[1]` and declared aggregate generated-child output members/items such as `producer.OUT_FRAME.tag` / `producer.OUT_FRAME.payload[1]`, with generated-child projections using one typed child-output carrier and list indexes lowering through the packed typedef `item_N` field convention before HDL emission.
  - The next bounded aggregate top-expression inference slice is now shipped too: declared or already inferred aggregate top-port paths such as `in_frame.tag` now contribute exact leaf widths during explicit-toplink concat inference, so a remaining omitted whole operand such as `payload` can be sized from the target remainder, same-block whole-root aggregate inference is order-independent inside the `?toplink` block, aggregate roots needed by explicit top expressions may also seed from unlinked same-name child inputs with one uniform record/list declared-type contract, and undeclared aggregate roots fail with a user-facing declared-root diagnostic instead of an internal exact-width assertion.
  - The matching composition aggregate-expression reporting hardening is now also shipped: provenance endpoint contexts now resolve those typed aggregate source paths to their leaf width/type facts and keep base declared type contracts when the structural IR already has them, so embedding/reporting surfaces no longer approximate `in_frame.tag` or `producer.OUT_FRAME.payload[1]` from packed base widths.
  - The matching aggregate-path ownership hardening now spans both direct and composition callers: [perl/FSM/Package/AggregatePathSupport.pm](perl/FSM/Package/AggregatePathSupport.pm) owns declared aggregate path traversal plus packed base-signal range projection, [perl/FSM/Composition/AggregatePathSupport.pm](perl/FSM/Composition/AggregatePathSupport.pm) wraps those path segments into structural connection expressions, direct [perl/FSM/Adapter/FSMGenFull/ExpressionBuilder.pm](perl/FSM/Adapter/FSMGenFull/ExpressionBuilder.pm) consumes the same resolver for `AggregateRef` AST construction, and [perl/FSM/Synthesis/EnableGraph/AssignmentSupport.pm](perl/FSM/Synthesis/EnableGraph/AssignmentSupport.pm) consumes the same packed-range resolver for partial aggregate LHS lowering, with [t/283-composition-aggregate-path-support.t](t/283-composition-aggregate-path-support.t) and [t/284-package-aggregate-path-support.t](t/284-package-aggregate-path-support.t) locking wrapper-level and package-level semantics.
  - The next direct pre-generation aggregate assignment hardening slice is now shipped too: partial aggregate LHS writes preserve per-assignment whole-aggregate source/target contracts through base-signal mux normalization, so compatible values like `TAIL` assigned to `OUT.payload` validate against the `payload` leaf contract while incompatible width-equal aggregate shapes still fail before emission with leaf-target diagnostics; this is now locked at both validator level and direct pipeline/CLI level, and the validator fallback delegates target-contract extraction back to the assignment-support owner.
  - The first direct RHS pack slice is now shipped: direct assignment expressions accept `(concat ...)` plus `(cat ...)`, preserve a `CoreAST::Concatenation` node, emit SystemVerilog concat text from that AST, feed the summed exact operand width into the pre-generation assignment-width contract so mismatches abort before emission with authored RHS diagnostics, and infer an ordered source aggregate/list/record contract when a direct RHS concat drives a declared aggregate target, including nested concat-as-list shape, so width-equal but shape/order-incompatible concat assignments are blocked before HDL emission.
  - The matching bounded direct LHS deconstruct slice is now shipped: direct assignment targets may use `(concat ...)` or `(cat ...)` over static whole-signal, bit/slice, or aggregate-leaf lvalues; authored left-to-right targets map onto high-to-low RHS slices; exact operand/RHS widths, exact total-width agreement, and non-overlapping target ranges are validated before generation; the enable-graph capture path lowers the deconstruct into ordinary normalized per-target assignments rather than leaving renderer-side concat semantics in the backend; whole-aggregate RHS roots now preserve exact nested source-fragment contracts during that split, including typed aggregate RHS signals and typed aggregate sub-roots instead of only named aggregate constants; RHS concat fragments that exactly align to whole concat operands now preserve those operands plus their typed aggregate contracts instead of hiding them behind a packed-blob part-select; and nested RHS concat fragments now preserve aligned nested operand list shape plus target-aware record member mapping, so compatible subaggregate fragments validate against the matching target fragment contract instead of being compared as the whole aggregate root or flattened to width-only behavior.
  - The CoreAST aggregate expression type-shape inference pocket now has a shared package owner in [perl/FSM/Package/AggregateExpressionTypeSupport.pm](perl/FSM/Package/AggregateExpressionTypeSupport.pm), and both the direct parser plus the EnableGraph capture path consume that owner with their local exact-width resolver instead of carrying parallel concat/list/record walkers.
  - The reset contract correction slice is now shipped: `sreset` records synchronous active-high reset semantics, canonical `areset` records asynchronous active-low reset semantics, direct SystemVerilog state/flop/pulse emission now consumes reset policy helpers instead of always emitting async active-low logic, and strict mode rejects misleading `(sreset rstn)` plus legacy `(asreset rstn)` while default mode keeps those compatibility forms.
  - The next honest aggregate-type seams are now broader inference-first aggregate access, deconstruct widening beyond the first bounded static-lvalue direct-assignment form, VHDL aggregate lowering, and public type/export surface stabilization rather than packed-width-only direct SystemVerilog declaration emission.
  - Future aggregate-assignment steering now shifts from basic direct LHS deconstruct semantics to wider aggregate/type-aware deconstruct forms: keep the current high-to-low mapping and pre-generation legality checks, but widen only when the frontend can still deliver normalized AST/IR assignments to backends rather than renderer-side concat semantics.
  - The next typed-composition compatibility slice is now also shipped: explicit port-to-port `?toplink` bindings now consume preserved `declared_type_name` / canonical `declared_type_spec` too, so width-equal but alias-incompatible endpoints are blocked with the same failure-summary surface instead of slipping through explicit-link planning.
  - The next typed-composition safety slice is now also shipped: same-name composition convention and undeclared top-port inference now consume preserved `declared_type_name` / canonical `declared_type_spec` directly, so width-equal but alias-incompatible child families are blocked instead of being silently flattened to width-only agreement, and inferred undeclared top ports now preserve one shared declared type contract when the child-side evidence is uniform.
